**Findings (critical / high / medium)**

1. **The driver API returns the child handover codes she is supposed to collect from the authorised adult** — 🟠 high · security · _confirmed_
   - Evidence: services/demo-api/src/services/prototype.ts:250-257 (family() returns full child objects to a driver whose trips reference the child); prototype.ts:79-91 (recipients[].verificationCode is stored in plain text on the child); prototype.ts:699-707 (completion only compares input.code with that same stored code); packages/ui/src/features/prototype.tsx:733-740 (the UI hides the code from drivers, but the API payload still contains it)
   - Detail: GET /prototype/family called with a driver token returns children[].recipients[].verificationCode. The 'Code de remise' step is the main safety check of Naya Famille, and any driver can read every code from her own responses with a proxy or debug build. The test at test/prototype.test.ts:153-156 calls this exact endpoint as the driver and does not notice the leak.
   - Fix: Return a driver-facing DTO without verificationCode (recipient id, name and relationship only). Store a hash of each code, add attempt limiting, and add a regression test that asserts no driver payload contains verificationCode.
2. **Cash cancellation fees stay 'pending' forever, are never collected, and the driver is never paid** — 🟠 high · bug · _confirmed_
   - Evidence: services/demo-api/src/services/rides.ts:439-462 (cash branch: payment pushed with status 'pending', provider 'cash', providerRef null; no ledger entry and no provider job); apps/passenger/src/app/(app)/receipt/[id].tsx:19 (copy says '… à régler avec votre prochaine course.'); createQuote and settleRide (rides.ts:70-106, 605-638) never add outstanding fees; test/scenarios.test.ts:276-295 asserts only the fee amount, not its settlement
   - Detail: When a cash passenger cancels after the grace period or after the driver arrived, the API records a cancellation_fee payment with no way to resolve it. The passenger is told the fee will be added to her next ride, which never happens. The driver who travelled gets nothing (wallet and card fees credit her, finance.ts:144-152 and rides.ts:457-460). Admin pending-payment lists (app.ts:835) grow without limit.
   - Fix: Decide the cash-fee policy (add it to the next fare, or take it from the driver's commission debt). Implement it with a ledger posting keyed ride:<id>:cancel-fee, or drop the fee for cash and fix the receipt copy. Add a test covering both the passenger and the driver side.
3. **Payers can confirm their own money, and these simulated-payment routes stay live with NAYA_DEV=0** — 🟡 medium · security · _adjusted_
   - Evidence: services/demo-api/src/services/prototype.ts:494-517 (POST /prototype/wallet/:id/resolve lets the passenger set her own top-up to 'confirmed'; no devMode check); prototype.ts:453-493 (top-up of up to 10,000,000 centimes, no provider job); app.ts:593-607 (POST /provider-sandbox/:kind/:ref/:outcome: the owner can approve her own recharge or card payment whatever devMode is); services/rides.ts:625-629 (wallet rides then post ride_net_credit to the driver's withdrawable ledger)
   - Detail: In the demo these endpoints stand in for hosted checkouts, which is intended. But they are not tied to dev or demo mode. With NAYA_DEV=0 a driver can still self-approve a recharge, and a passenger can still mint wallet balance or approve a pending card payment. The resulting value flows into driver ledgers that can be withdrawn. The passenger wallet also skips the providerJobs/processedProviderEvents/signed-callback pipeline that driver recharges use.
   - Fix: Register the sandbox, tokenize and wallet-resolve routes only when ctx.config.devMode (or an explicit providerMode==='demo') is set. Route passenger top-ups through queueProviderJob/applyProviderEventIn like driver recharges, and document these routes as non-portable.
   - Verifier: The facts hold. /prototype/wallet/:id/resolve (prototype.ts:494-517) has no devMode check. A top-up can be up to 10,000,000 centimes with no provider job (prototype.ts:453-493). /provider-sandbox (app.ts:593-607) lets the owner approve her own recharge or payment even with NAYA_DEV=0, and wallet rides then post ride_net_credit (rides.ts:625-628). Severity is lowered because all money in this serv…
4. **SOS alerts can only be seen and handled with config.edit; the support agent role gets 403** — 🟡 medium · product · _adjusted_
   - Evidence: services/demo-api/src/services/prototype.ts:877-878 (GET /admin/prototype requires config.edit), :926-928 (POST /admin/prototype/alerts/:id requires config.edit); seed.ts:301 (the support agent AD-002 has only support.resolve, rides.read, people.read)
   - Detail: Safety alerts are an operations and support duty, but only the all-permissions admin can see or resolve them. The same catch-all permission also exposes all children's PII (names, schools, handover codes) and every passenger wallet to config.edit holders, while finance.read holders cannot see passenger wallets at all.
   - Fix: Add dedicated permissions (for example safety.respond, family.read, finance.read for passenger wallets) and split /admin/prototype into domain endpoints. Give the support role SOS access, and add a route-by-role authorization test.
   - Verifier: Verified. GET /admin/prototype (prototype.ts:877-878) and POST /admin/prototype/alerts/:id (926-928) require config.edit. AD-002 has only support.resolve, rides.read and people.read (seed.ts:301). The only place SOS appears in the admin is apps/admin/src/pages/Prototype.tsx. Lowered to medium because SOS is labelled as a simulation end to end (it has no real dispatch or notifications) and the see…
5. **Driver earnings 'Crédité au portefeuille' counts card rides only; wallet and mobile-wallet credits are missing** — 🟡 medium · bug · _confirmed_
   - Evidence: services/demo-api/src/services/finance.ts:276-277 (walletCredited and pendingElectronic filter x.method === 'card'); services/rides.ts:625-629 (wallet and mobile_wallet rides post ride_net_credit); apps/driver/src/app/(app)/(tabs)/earnings.tsx:59-63 (tile labelled 'Courses carte (net)')
   - Detail: Since Oct 6, wallet and mobile_wallet rides credit the driver ledger, but the earnings summary leaves them out of both cashCollected and walletCredited. The tiles therefore don't add up to net, and drivers under-read their credited income.
   - Fix: Treat every non-cash method as electronic (method !== 'cash') in earningsOf, rename the tile copy, and add a test with mixed payment methods.
6. **Dev mode is on by default, the server binds 0.0.0.0, /dev routes have no authentication, and CORS reflects any origin** — 🟡 medium · security · _confirmed_
   - Evidence: services/demo-api/src/main.ts:13 (devMode = NAYA_DEV !== '0'), :45 (hostname '0.0.0.0'); app.ts:202 (cors origin (o) => o ?? '*'); app.ts:895-988 (/dev/reset, /dev/clock/advance, /dev/providers/:kind/:ref/:outcome, /dev/bot, /dev/navigate: no principal check); services/auth.ts:35,51 (fixed OTP plus demoCode in the response)
   - Detail: Any process that runs `pnpm api` without NAYA_DEV=0 lets anyone on the network sign in as any phone number, wipe the data, move the clock, confirm any payment, and push sign-in and navigation commands to review builds through /dev/navigate. Any web page open in the developer's browser can also call these localhost routes, because the CORS preflight passes.
   - Fix: Default devMode to off unless NAYA_DEV=1, bind to 127.0.0.1 by default, require a dev token or admin principal on /dev/*, and restrict CORS to configured origins.
7. **Reusing a correction Idempotency-Key with a different body silently returns the old entry; keys are global** — 🟡 medium · bug · _confirmed_
   - Evidence: services/demo-api/src/services/finance.ts:284-299 (ledger key `correction:${idempotencyKey}`, no body hash, no admin scoping; on a duplicate it returns the old entry); finance.ts:290 (nextId('CO') is used up even on duplicates); docs/FINANCE.md:55 (claims 'different body is refused'); app.ts:851-857 (separate code path from idempotent())
   - Detail: An admin who retries a correction for a different driver or amount with the same key gets HTTP 200 and somebody else's entry, with no error. Keys are also shared across admins. relatedEntityId from correctionSchema is validated but never stored, and the amount has no upper bound beyond a safe integer.
   - Fix: Use the shared idempotent() helper (subject = admin id), store relatedEntityId, cap the amount, and add a conflict test.
8. **Any prototype catalog edit forcibly re-enables or disables derived recharge providers, with no audit entry** — 🟡 medium · bug · _confirmed_
   - Evidence: services/demo-api/src/services/prototype.ts:913 (prototypeProviders(s, true) on every PUT /admin/prototype/catalog/:kind); prototype.ts:213-238 (force resets existing.enabled from the catalog); services/cities.ts:145-153 (audited provider toggle that gets overridden)
   - Detail: An admin disables 'rabat-demo-card-recharge' on the Providers page (audited). A later edit to an unrelated dispute reason or family plan silently re-enables it. GET /payment-providers also creates providers outside a transaction and before authentication (app.ts:563-565).
   - Fix: Only create missing providers; never override enabled on existing ones. Move provider sync into the catalog payments upsert only and audit any change.
9. **Prototype admin changes skip the config conventions: no reason, no before/after, no version check** — 🟡 medium · architecture · _confirmed_
   - Evidence: services/demo-api/src/services/prototype.ts:269-289 (log() records summary only, entityType 'prototype'); :890-925 (catalog upsert with no reasonSchema and no expectedVersion although catalog.version exists); :949-998 (family reassignment), :999-1026 (driver categories); compare services/cities.ts:78-106 (city rules require reason plus expectedVersion and record before/after)
   - Detail: Category commission and fare changes, plan prices, payment options and driver eligibility are money and safety configuration. Unlike city rules, they can be changed without a reason, with lost-update risk, and leave an audit trail that cannot reconstruct what changed.
   - Fix: Reuse the reason and expectedVersion pattern from updateRules, record before/after, and version categories alongside cityRuleVersions.
10. **Financial aggregates and driver balances are visible without finance.read** — 🟡 medium · security · _confirmed_
   - Evidence: services/demo-api/src/app.ts:647-656 (/admin/overview checks only adminOf; returns volume, commission, byMethod); app.ts:688 (/admin/people list returns walletOf for every driver, while the detail view at :700/:706 checks finance.read); app.ts:859-863, 872-876 (/admin/cities and /admin/providers have no permission check)
   - Detail: The support agent role (seed.ts:301) can read revenue, commission and every driver's balance and debt through the overview and the people list. That contradicts the finance.read gating used elsewhere.
   - Fix: Gate the financial fields on finance.read (omit them or return null), add config.read for cities and providers, and cover this in an authorization-matrix test.
11. **A driver who was only offered a ride can see the other driver, her plate, and the passenger's rating comment** — 🟡 medium · security · _confirmed_
   - Evidence: services/demo-api/src/app.ts:434-440 (for an unassigned driver the response is { ...ride, ... } and still includes driver, driverId, rating, driverCancellations, cancellation)
   - Detail: The 'own involvement only' comment at app.ts:437 is not enforced. Spreading the full ride exposes another driver's name, vehicle and plate, the passenger's rating and comment about that driver, and other drivers' cancellation reasons.
   - Fix: Build an explicit DriverRideView DTO (route, terms, own offer, own cancellation) instead of spreading the entity.
12. **Verified users can rename themselves and drivers can change city through PATCH /me, with no audit** — 🟡 medium · product · _confirmed_
   - Evidence: services/demo-api/src/app.ts:257-271 (firstName, lastName and cityId writable at any time; no appendAudit); services/rides.ts:233-243 (driver name shown to passengers comes from the user record); services/finance.ts:29-41 (walletOf uses the driver's current city's debtLimit)
   - Detail: On a women-only safety service, the name shown to the other party after manual identity review can be changed freely. A driver can also move herself to a city with a higher debt limit, or change city mid-ride.
   - Fix: Lock identity fields after approval (changes go through a new verification case), require admin action for a driver city change, and audit profile changes.
13. **No API to create payout accounts, so newly registered drivers can never withdraw** — 🟡 medium · product · _confirmed_
   - Evidence: services/demo-api/src/seed.ts:340-343 is the only place s.payoutAccounts is written (grep finds no route); services/finance.ts:218 (withdrawal requires an existing payout account)
   - Detail: STANDALONE-DEMO.md tells testers any phone number can register. Those drivers reach the withdrawal flow with no destination account.
   - Fix: Add payout-account CRUD (with RIB validation and verification status) or seed one simulated account on driver approval.
14. **No account deletion, user suspension, admin ride cancel/refund, or passenger refund** — 🟡 medium · product · _confirmed_
   - Evidence: packages/domain/src/entities.ts:39 (status 'suspended' exists) but no route sets it; packages/domain/src/machines/ride.ts:20-22 (SYSTEM_CANCEL unused); services/support.ts:79-92 (resolution is text only); app.ts has no DELETE /me
   - Detail: Support can resolve a dispute only with words; there is no money remedy except driver-side corrections. Ops cannot suspend a dangerous user or cancel a stuck ride. App Store guidelines require in-app account deletion for apps that create accounts.
   - Fix: Add suspension (with reason and audit), admin system-cancel, passenger refund and credit postings tied to tickets, and an account-deletion request flow.
15. **A passenger with an unpaid failed card payment or an unpaid fee can keep booking** — 🟡 medium · product · _confirmed_
   - Evidence: services/demo-api/src/services/rides.ts:190-196 (createRide checks verification and active ride only); rides.ts:528-546 (retry allows only cards; failed payments remain)
   - Detail: The driver of a failed card ride gets nothing until the passenger retries, and nothing pushes the passenger to settle.
   - Fix: Block new bookings while a ride or fee payment is failed or pending past a threshold, and offer cash or wallet settlement.
16. **Driver category restrictions silently stop offers while the status endpoint reports 'eligible'** — 🟡 medium · ux · _confirmed_
   - Evidence: services/demo-api/src/services/rides.ts:262 (dispatch filters by driverCategories) and :370 (accept refuses); rides.ts:215-231 (driverEligibility has no category reason); prototype.ts:1012-1014 (an empty categoryIds array is allowed)
   - Detail: An admin can set categories to [] or to ones with no demand. The driver shows as online and eligible but never receives offers. The default (no entry) allows every category, so car drivers also receive scooter rides.
   - Fix: Add a 'no_categories' eligibility reason, reject empty arrays, and decide whether the default should follow the vehicle type.
17. **Naya Famille is a parallel model with no payment, dispatch, eligibility, zone or ledger link** — 🟡 medium · architecture · _confirmed_
   - Evidence: services/demo-api/src/services/prototype.ts:303-319 (the dedicated driver is the first verified driver in the city), :343-350 ('paiement simulé', but no wallet debit or payment record), :353-427 (addTrip has no zone or lead-time check and no driver availability or eligibility check), :521-532 (children can be added but not edited or deleted; recipients cannot be revoked); FamilyTrip has no fare or ledger fields (packages/domain/src/prototype.ts:90-109)
   - Detail: Subscriptions are activated without any payment. Drivers are never paid for family trips. A suspended or debt-blocked dedicated driver still runs trips. An authorised adult cannot be revoked (for example after a custody change).
   - Fix: Write an ADR for whether family trips are rides (reuse dispatch, payments and ledger) or a separate product. At minimum: charge the subscription to the wallet, check driver eligibility at trip time, and add child and recipient edit/revoke with audit.
18. **Child pickup 'verification' is retyping the name the driver already has; the 4-digit handover code has no attempt limit** — 🟡 medium · product · _confirmed_
   - Evidence: services/demo-api/src/services/prototype.ts:688-697 (pickupVerified=true when childName matches the name already in the trip and child payload); :699-707 (no attempt counter or lockout); :85-88 (4 digits, stored in plain text)
   - Detail: These steps are presented as safety confirmations, but they can be passed by brute force (10,000 combinations, no lockout) or with information the driver already holds.
   - Fix: Count failed handover attempts and lock the trip (alerting the parent and support), hash the codes, and frame the name step as an attestation rather than a verification in the copy.
19. **Passenger wallet entries are changed in place and the balance is a stored field updated by GET handlers** — 🟡 medium · architecture · _confirmed_
   - Evidence: services/demo-api/src/services/prototype.ts:159-184 (settle rewrites hold.amount, label, at and status), :106-118 (passengerWallet recomputes and writes balance/reserved, and creates the wallet on read); services/rides.ts:798 (scheduled hold id and rideId re-keyed in place); app.ts:360 and prototype.ts:450-452 (called outside any tx)
   - Detail: Unlike the driver ledger, the passenger wallet is not append-only and has no business idempotency keys. It cannot be audited or ported as-is, and GET requests mutate state outside transactions.
   - Fix: Model the passenger wallet as an append-only ledger (hold, capture and release entries with unique keys) projected the same way as the driver wallet.
20. **Every transaction deep-clones and rewrites the whole state, and the idempotency cache stores full responses** — 🟡 medium · performance · _confirmed_
   - Evidence: services/demo-api/src/store.ts:33 (JSON clone per tx), :61-67 (full JSON write per tx); app.ts:140-141 (up to 2,000 idempotency records with full ride responses); services/rides.ts:102 (500 quotes with polylines); audit, offers, providerJobs and processedProviderEvents grow without limit (finance.ts:98, 166)
   - Detail: In standalone TestFlight builds this runs on the JS thread for every request (embedded.ts:69 calls tick, which may also run a tx). Cost grows with history, causing latency and jank over weeks of tester use.
   - Fix: Store response hashes or ids instead of bodies, prune delivered jobs and old events, cap the audit on device, or move the embedded store to SQLite.
21. **Persisted state has no migrations: schemaVersion is always 1 and catalog defaults never reach existing data** — 🟡 medium · maintainability · _confirmed_
   - Evidence: services/demo-api/src/store.ts:22-30 (schemaVersion===1, otherwise reseed); state.ts:87-88 (prototype optional, created lazily by ensurePrototype at prototype.ts:95-105); embedded.ts:17 (demo-v1.json on tester devices)
   - Detail: Oct 6 fields were added as optional, but any new required field or changed shape will either crash old TestFlight data or need a silent reseed. New default categories, plans or reasons never appear in a device that already has a catalog.
   - Fix: Add numbered migrations run in Store.load and bump schemaVersion on shape changes. In the port this becomes SQL migrations.
22. **Several sensitive mutations write no audit event** — 🟡 medium · architecture · _confirmed_
   - Evidence: services/demo-api/src/services/rides.ts:464-488 (passenger cancel/fee), :349-384 (assignment), :714-782 (scheduled create/modify/cancel); app.ts:257-271 (profile name/city), :365-408 (payment methods); services/auth.ts:133-135 (failed admin logins); audit.ts:8-12 (hash excludes the id field; no key or anchoring)
   - Detail: Disputes about fees or identity changes cannot be reconstructed from the audit. The chain only detects partial edits: anyone with write access can rehash the whole file.
   - Fix: Define an audit policy (which actions must be audited), add the missing events, and in the port anchor the chain (periodic signed checkpoints, or a WORM table).
23. **No tests for the NAYA_DEV=0 configuration, the authorization matrix, or several Oct 6 money and safety paths** — 🟡 medium · testing · _confirmed_
   - Evidence: services/demo-api/test/harness.ts:22 (devMode always true), :30 (an Idempotency-Key is added to every POST automatically); test/scenarios.test.ts:591-602 ('authorizes every operation by role' covers 6 routes); :276-295 (cash fee settlement unchecked), :637-640 (weak either/or assertion); test/prototype.test.ts:153-156 (driver family payload not checked for codes)
   - Detail: Untested: sandbox and wallet-resolve gating, support-agent 403s on prototype admin routes, earnings for wallet rides, catalog edits toggling providers, scheduled wallet holds being re-keyed, family recurrence timezone, malformed JSON returning 500, the provider-job poison case.
   - Fix: Add a table-driven route-by-principal authorization test, a devMode=false harness variant, and regression tests for each issue above.
24. **A failed card cancellation fee cannot be retried; the receipt tells the passenger to pay with another card but offers no action** — 🟡 medium · bug · _found-by-verifier_
   - Evidence: services/demo-api/src/services/rides.ts:439-462 (chargeFee pushes the fee payment but never sets ride.paymentId; only settleRide sets it, at :624); rides.ts:531-532 (retryPayment looks up ride.paymentId and throws 'Aucun paiement à relancer' if it is missing); apps/passenger/src/app/(app)/receipt/[id].tsx:22 (failed state message 'Réglez avec une autre carte.'), :66 (a cancelled ride uses the fee payment for the banner), :97 (the retry action shows only when main?.status === 'failed' && !cancelled); finance.ts:144-152 (the driver is credited only when the fee payment is confirmed)
   - Detail: With test card 0002, or any declined card, the cancellation fee payment becomes 'failed'. The passenger sees 'Paiement refusé … Réglez avec une autre carte.' with no button. The API also rejects a retry because the fee is not the ride's paymentId. The driver who travelled to the pickup is never compensated, and the payment remains 'failed' in admin lists. Together with the cash fee finding, this means only wallet fees are ever settled.
   - Fix: Model fees as their own payable (a feePaymentId on the ride, or retries keyed by payment id rather than ride). Expose retry for any failed payment owned by the payer, and add a test for failed-fee retry that checks the driver credit.
25. **Cash rides post the commission debt at completion, but the driver can only confirm full payment; there is no 'passenger did not pay' or partial path** — 🟡 medium · product · _found-by-verifier_
   - Evidence: services/demo-api/src/services/rides.ts:634-637 (settleRide posts the cash commission debit immediately); rides.ts:640-653 (confirmCashCollected requires amount === p.amount and is the only way to resolve the payment); app.ts:513-516 (the only cash route is /cash-collected); app.ts:174 (awaitingCashRide stays set indefinitely)
   - Detail: If a passenger leaves without paying, or pays less, the driver cannot record it. The commission is already counted as debt on her wallet and can push her toward the debt limit that blocks offers (rides.ts:227-228). The passenger is never flagged, and support has no money remedy (support.ts:79-92). Cash is the main payment method at launch, so this is a core settlement gap.
   - Fix: Add a 'cash_not_received' or partial-amount outcome. It should open a dispute, reverse or hold the commission debit with a keyed ledger entry, and flag the passenger account (and feed into the outstanding-payment booking block). Cover it with a scenario test.
26. **Parents cannot cancel or reschedule a family trip, recurrences are generated automatically, and drivers can advance a trip at any time** — 🟡 medium · product · _found-by-verifier_
   - Evidence: services/demo-api/src/services/prototype.ts:518-789 (the only family routes are GET family, children, subscribe, trips, example, trips/:id/advance and trips/:id/incident; there is no cancel or edit); prototype.ts:630-764 (advance has no check against t.pickupAt, so a driver can mark en_route or arrived days early); prototype.ts:731-760 (completion auto-creates the next weekday occurrence); packages/domain/src/prototype.ts:79 (subscription status 'paused' exists but no route sets it)
   - Detail: If a child is sick or the schedule changes, the parent has no way to stop a planned pickup, and each completed trip schedules the next one automatically until the included-trip quota runs out. Because advance has no timing check, the parent's notifications timeline can show 'Chauffeuse arrivée' at the wrong time. For a child-transport product, cancellation and pause are core flows.
   - Fix: Add cancel and reschedule for trips and pause or resume for subscriptions, with audit and driver notification. Gate advance on a time window around pickupAt, and add tests for cancel and recurrence.

**Open questions raised**

- Is the demo API ever deployed beyond localhost (shared-server mode EXPO_PUBLIC_STANDALONE=0, the web admin demo)? That decides how urgent the fail-open dev defaults and seeded admin passwords are.
- Which role should handle SOS alerts in operations: support agents or config admins? And should children's PII need its own permission?
- Policy for cash cancellation fees: add them to the next fare, take them from driver debt, or drop them for cash?
- Should Naya Famille subscriptions be charged (wallet or card), and are family trips rides that go through dispatch, ledger and earnings, or a separate product?
- Should admins be scoped to cities (Rabat vs Casablanca operations teams)?
- How is the women-only rule attested during verification? identityDetailsSchema has no sex/gender field and decisions record no eligibility checklist (packages/domain/src/schemas.ts:56-62).
- Target production stack for the port (Postgres plus Hono, or another framework)? Should the Hono routes and the packages/api contract be kept verbatim?
- Should the Next.js /backoffice (apps/web) be connected to this API, or stay a separate prototype?
- Should driver categories default to every category, or follow the vehicle type (scooter vs car)?
- Typecheck and tests were not run during this audit (read-only constraints); the 51/51 pass claim in docs/MOBILE-FEEDBACK-DEMO.md:40 was not re-verified.

<a id="quality"></a>

## Quality gates, tests, tooling and repo health

**Health 5/10.** Every local quality gate is green. `pnpm -r --parallel typecheck` passes all 10 packages in 11.5 s. Domain vitest passes 36/36, demo-api vitest 51/51, passenger jest 15/15 (one suite, shared UI only) and the web node:test 4/4. `expo lint` reports 0 errors and 5 warnings in each of passenger and driver. The process around those gates is weak. There is no CI on the GitHub remote (ProgixDev/naya). HEAD's committed pnpm-lock.yaml does not match apps/passenger/package.json: eslint was added in 89aae18 but the lockfile fix is still uncommitted. Root `pnpm test` pulls in Playwright e2e, so it fails without four running servers. Lint covers only the two Expo apps; applying the same config to packages/ui, admin and the API gives 22 errors. The full 40-test e2e suite has not been re-run since the Oct 6 changes: the last report holds one test. The new features (categories, dispute evidence, Services admin, wallet-paid rides) have little or no e2e or component coverage. Repo hygiene: ~95% of git history (257 MB of 271 MB) is images, docs/screens alone is 170 MB tracked and 216 MB across history, and a stray unrelated image set plus a 13 MB zip sit untracked at the root. apps/web is managed by both npm and pnpm, with drifting locks and a 458 MB `.ignored` folder. The root .env from .env.example is never loaded by any tool. For the user's goal of self-contained context: there is no root CLAUDE.md or AGENTS.md (Expo guidance is in AGENTS.expo.md, which no tool auto-loads), no ADR or spec folder, the source-of-truth `_handoff/` is git-ignored, and README, SCREENS, FINANCE, INTEGRATIONS and STANDALONE-DEMO all predate or contradict the Oct 6 state.

**Strengths**

- All 10 workspace typechecks pass under strict mode with noUncheckedIndexedAccess (`pnpm -r --parallel typecheck`, exit 0, 11.5 s).
- 106 automated unit and integration tests pass in under 20 s total (domain 36, demo-api 51, UI 15, web 4).
- Demo API tests are deterministic: in-memory Store, ManualClock fixed at 2026-10-01T08:00Z, automatic Idempotency-Keys, fixed OTP (services/demo-api/test/harness.ts). They cover wallet invariants, races, permissions, audit chain, standalone mode with network blocked, and the Oct 6 prototype workflows.
- Dependency versions are aligned across all apps (react 19.2.3, react-query 5.104.0, zod 4.6.5, tailwind 3.4.19, TS 6.0.3). Only one copy of react, react-native, react-query and app-level zod is installed (verified by scanning node_modules).
- The e2e suite is well structured: role-based projects, shared helpers that inject sessions, testID selectors (all 151 IDs resolve to source, literal or template), fr-MA locale and Africa/Casablanca timezone.
- No real secrets committed: no API keys, tokens or signing files are tracked. `.env*.local`, `*.p8`, `*.p12`, `*.key`, `*.mobileprovision` are ignored (apps/*/.gitignore). The Google Maps key is build-time env only.
- Both mobile apps lint with 0 errors.
- The README documents hard-won native toolchain quirks: signing for secure-store, `.env.production.local` for Release bundles, prebuild resetting Pods.

**Findings (critical / high / medium)**

1. **No root CLAUDE.md or AGENTS.md, no ADRs or specs, and the source-of-truth handoff is git-ignored** — 🟠 high · docs · _confirmed_
   - Evidence: Root CLAUDE.md, AGENTS.md, docs/adr and docs/decisions are all absent. Expo guidance lives in AGENTS.expo.md, which neither Claude Code nor Codex auto-loads by that name. apps/web/CLAUDE.md is only `@AGENTS.md` (Next.js boilerplate). .gitignore:13 `_handoff/` ignores Naya-Expo-React-Native-Implementation-Prompt.md, QA.md and project-manifest.json, yet README.md:7 cites them as the source of truth.
   - Detail: Every new Claude or Codex session has to rediscover architecture, conventions, gates and the Oct 6 state from a 190-line README and seven dated reports. A fresh clone cannot see the original spec or QA checklist at all. This is exactly the context gap the user wants closed.
   - Fix: Create a root AGENTS.md and a CLAUDE.md that imports it, under 200 lines: purpose, layout, commands, gates and baselines, env flags, invariants, gotchas, and links. Add docs/README.md as an index, docs/adr/NNNN-*.md (see adr_candidates), and docs/specs/ with the product and screen spec. Commit text parts of _handoff (QA.md, project-manifest.json, implementation prompt) into docs/specs/, or record where they live.
2. **No CI pipeline; quality gates run only by hand** — 🟡 medium · dx · _adjusted_
   - Evidence: `.github`, `.gitlab-ci.yml` and `.circleci` are all absent. `git remote -v` → origin https://github.com/ProgixDev/naya.git, and master is pushed (0 ahead/0 behind). There are 20 commits, all verified manually as described in README.md:162-179 and docs/MOBILE-FEEDBACK-DEMO.md:33-46.
   - Detail: Typecheck, unit/API tests, lint and lockfile integrity are not enforced on push or PR. This already let a broken lockfile and stale verification claims onto master. A large share of the codebase is generated by AI tools with long lines and little review surface, which makes automated gates more important.
   - Fix: Add a GitHub Actions workflow on Node 24 and pnpm 12.8.1 that runs `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile`, `pnpm typecheck`, unit tests (domain, demo-api, passenger jest, web node:test), lint, and `pnpm peers check`. Add a second, optional job that boots the demo API plus the Expo web and Vite servers and runs Playwright.
   - Verifier: Confirmed: there is no .github, .gitlab-ci.yml, .circleci or hook tooling (.husky), and the only tracked deployment config is eas.json in each app. Corrections: master is not '0 ahead/0 behind'. It is behind origin by 1 commit (02ff3d3), which a second contributor pushed straight to master. That commit mixes a lockfile fix with unrelated iOS build-number bumps under a 'chore: enable unrs-resolver…
3. **`pnpm test` also runs Playwright e2e, which needs four servers, so the documented unit-test command fails** — 🟡 medium · dx · _confirmed_
   - Evidence: package.json:17 `"test": "pnpm -r --if-present test"`. e2e/package.json:6 `"test": "playwright test"`. e2e/playwright.config.ts:10-13 expects servers on 4010, 8081, 8082 and 5173 with no webServer block. README.md:153 says `pnpm test` covers domain unit tests, API scenario tests and component interaction tests.
   - Detail: Developers and agents following the README see a red run (or a long wait) unless the API, both Expo web servers and admin are running. pnpm -r bails on the first failure, which can hide the real unit-test results.
   - Fix: Make the root `test` script filter to unit packages, e.g. `pnpm -r --filter '!@naya/e2e' --if-present test`, keep `pnpm e2e` separate, and fix README.md:153.
4. **Full e2e suite not re-run after the Oct 6 change set** — 🟡 medium · testing · _confirmed_
   - Evidence: Decoded e2e/playwright-report/index.html (Oct 6 06:06): stats {total: 1}, the only test is scenarios-feedback.spec.ts. docs/MOBILE-FEEDBACK-DEMO.md:43 lists only 'Five scenarios passed' as selected regressions. Commit 89aae18 rewrote booking, support and ride flows (e.g. packages/ui/src/features/support.tsx +39, CancelSheet, SchedulePicker). README.md:170 still claims 39/39.
   - Detail: 34 of the 40 e2e tests have not been executed against the current code, so regressions in the core S01–S16 flows would go unnoticed. No stale testIDs were found statically, but behavior was not verified.
   - Fix: Run the full `pnpm e2e` against fresh servers, record the result with a date in docs, and fix any failures before further feature work. Add it to CI as a nightly or optional job.
5. **Oct 6 features largely uncovered by e2e** — 🟡 medium · testing · _confirmed_
   - Evidence: Grepping e2e/tests for Confort\|Scooter\|catégor finds only an unrelated `category-payment` support pill (passenger-flows.spec.ts:217). No setInputFiles or evidence uploads anywhere. No admin 'Services' catalog edits: scenarios-feedback.spec.ts:69 only opens the SOS tab and :121 the logo tab. A route-reference scan finds no test reference for POST /prototype/family/trips/:id/incident, POST /admin/prototype/assignment/:id, POST /prototype/family/children or POST /prototype/family/subscribe (services/demo-api/src/services/prototype.ts:521,533,765,949). 40 of 125 demo-api routes have no literal reference in API or e2e tests.
   - Detail: Service-category selection and price comparison, dispute evidence (image or PDF, required-evidence reasons, admin rejection), the Services admin page (categories, plans, reasons, payment options, driver eligibility, family assignment), wallet-paid ride hold and settlement, refused top-ups, passenger-side SOS, and family negative paths (wrong child name or handover code) have API tests at best and no UI-level coverage.
   - Fix: Split scenarios-feedback.spec.ts into focused specs: categories, wallet, family-happy, family-negative, SOS, disputes-evidence, admin-services. Add API tests for incident reporting and admin family assignment.
6. **Lint covers only the two Expo apps; shared UI, admin and API code has 22 hidden errors** — 🟡 medium · maintainability · _confirmed_
   - Evidence: Only apps/passenger/package.json:12 and apps/driver/package.json:12 define `lint`, and the root has no lint script. Running `node_modules/.bin/eslint -c apps/passenger/eslint.config.js packages/ui/src services/demo-api/src packages/domain/src packages/api/src apps/admin/src` → '✖ 32 problems (22 errors, 10 warnings)'. Examples: packages/ui/src/map/useAnimatedCoordinate.ts:10 react-hooks/set-state-in-effect; packages/ui/src/map/TileMap.tsx:104-106 react-hooks/refs; packages/ui/src/Feedback.tsx:125 react-hooks/refs; packages/ui/src/BottomSheet.tsx:53,57,133,146 react-hooks/immutability; apps/admin/src/components/DocViewer.tsx:39 and apps/admin/src/pages/Rules.tsx:123 set-state-in-effect.
   - Detail: packages/ui (4,956 lines, the core of both apps) and the admin are never linted, so React hooks and compiler rule violations accumulate unseen. Some are false positives (Reanimated shared values), but nobody triages them.
   - Fix: Add a root flat ESLint config covering apps, packages and services, with an RN/Expo preset for mobile and a React/TS preset for admin and API. Add a root `lint` script, triage the 22 errors (suppress the Reanimated false positives explicitly), and run it in CI.
7. **Screenshots dominate the repository: 170 MB tracked, ~216 MB of history in docs/screens** — 🟡 medium · maintainability · _confirmed_
   - Evidence: `git count-objects -vH` → size-pack: 222.62 MiB. Tracked total 205,312 KB (`git ls-files -z \| xargs -0 du -ck`), with 424 png and 79 jpg. docs/screens has 421 files and 170,200 KB (native 92 MB, driver 35 MB, passenger 32 MB, admin 9 MB). Blob scan: image blobs total 256.8 MB of 271.1 MB on disk, and docs/screens accounts for 591 blobs / 216.5 MB across history. Screens were re-committed in 9 of 20 commits.
   - Detail: Clones are slow, AI and code tools waste time indexing images, and every capture refresh adds tens of MB permanently. Most native captures predate the Oct 6 redesign anyway (only docs/screens/feedback was refreshed).
   - Fix: Move docs/screens to Git LFS or external artifact storage (GitHub release assets, Drive) and keep a small curated set in docs. Consider a one-time history rewrite (git filter-repo) if the team agrees. Add `docs/screens/**/*.png` to LFS tracking.
8. **apps/web is managed by both pnpm and npm, with drifting lockfiles** — 🟡 medium · dx · _confirmed_
   - Evidence: pnpm-workspace.yaml:2 includes apps/*, so pnpm-lock.yaml:616 has an `apps/web:` importer. apps/web/package-lock.json is also tracked (75 KB). Root package.json:22-23 runs `npm run dev/build --prefix apps/web`. README.md:190 and apps/web/README.md:7,11-13 recommend npm. Lock drift: lucide-react npm 1.52.0 vs pnpm 1.49.0, postcss 8.5.29 vs 8.5.28, @types/node 24.19.1 vs 24.19.0. A previous npm install was moved by pnpm into apps/web/node_modules/.ignored (458 MB).
   - Detail: Two sources of truth for web dependencies, wasted disk space, and an ambiguous root `build` script (it builds only the marketing site, not the admin or API).
   - Fix: Pick pnpm. Delete apps/web/package-lock.json and apps/web/node_modules/.ignored, rename the root scripts (`web`, `web:build`, `admin:build`), and update both READMEs. If Vercel needs a standalone install, configure it for the pnpm workspace.
9. **The root .env suggested by README/.env.example is never read by any tool** — 🟡 medium · dx · _confirmed_
   - Evidence: README.md:62 says `cp .env.example .env`, and .env.example contains NAYA_DEV, NAYA_PROVIDER_SECRET, EXPO_PUBLIC_* and VITE_API_URL. services/demo-api/package.json `"dev": "tsx watch src/main.ts"` has no --env-file or dotenv, and `git grep dotenv\|env-file\|envDir\|loadEnv` finds nothing. Vite's default envDir is apps/admin (apps/admin/vite.config.ts has no envDir). Expo loads only `path.join(projectRoot, envFile)` (node_modules/@expo/env/build/index.js:313-324), i.e. apps/<app>/.env.
   - Detail: Someone who sets NAYA_DEV=0 or a real NAYA_PROVIDER_SECRET in the root .env believes they hardened the API, but services/demo-api/src/main.ts:13 keeps dev mode on (fixed OTP 123456, /dev routes), main.ts:15 keeps the default secret, and the server binds 0.0.0.0 (main.ts:45) with CORS reflecting any origin (app.ts:202).
   - Fix: Either load the root env explicitly (`tsx --env-file=../../.env`, Vite `envDir: '../../'`, or per-app .env symlinks), or rewrite .env.example and README to state which file each process reads. Consider making devMode opt-in (`NAYA_DEV === '1'`).
10. **The iOS paths-with-spaces fix is uncommitted and applied to passenger only** — 🟡 medium · dx · _confirmed_
   - Evidence: `git status`: `?? plugins/with-ios-build-paths.js` and ` M apps/passenger/app.config.ts` (adds appleTeamId '89884BGNZR' and '../../plugins/with-ios-build-paths'). apps/driver/app.config.ts:30-33 plugins lack it. `grep -c NAYA_CONSTANTS_SCRIPT apps/driver/ios/Podfile` → 0 vs 2 in passenger. The repo path contains spaces ('/Volumes/UNREAL/Codex Space Recovery/...'). The plugin rewrites Xcode shell phases via regex and JSON.parse (plugins/with-ios-build-paths.js:38-49).
   - Detail: Committing app.config.ts without the plugin breaks prebuild for others. The driver app will hit the same EXConstants and bundle-script failure on this machine. The regex rewrite is fragile across Expo SDK upgrades.
   - Fix: Commit the plugin and config together and add the plugin to the driver config, with appleTeamId taken from env or app config consistently. Document in AGENTS.md why it exists and when it can be removed (once Expo quotes paths). Alternatively, keep the repo on a path without spaces.
11. **Shared packages rely on hoisting for undeclared dependencies and reach into other packages' internals** — 🟡 medium · architecture · _confirmed_
   - Evidence: packages/ui/package.json:10-15 declares only @naya/assets, demo-api, domain and tokens, yet packages/ui/src imports @naya/api (23 imports), react-native (39), react (31), @tanstack/react-query (11), lucide-react-native (11), zustand and ~20 expo-* modules. services/demo-api/src/platform.native.ts:3 imports expo-crypto and src/fixtures.native.ts imports expo-asset, neither declared in services/demo-api/package.json. packages/ui/src/core/localDemo.native.ts:3 imports '../../../../services/demo-api/src/fixtures.native', bypassing package exports. packages/ui/tsconfig.json:4 includes '../../apps/passenger/nativewind-env.d.ts', so a shared package depends on an app.
   - Detail: Everything works only because pnpm-workspace.yaml:8 sets nodeLinker: hoisted. Moving to isolated linking, extracting a package, or adding a third app breaks in non-obvious ways, and dependency ownership is invisible to tools such as depcheck and Renovate.
   - Fix: Declare peerDependencies (react, react-native, expo modules, react-query, zustand, lucide) and the @naya/api dependency in packages/ui. Export fixtures.native via the demo-api `exports` map. Move nativewind-env.d.ts into packages/ui or a shared types package.
12. **The full demo backend, seed credentials and fixtures are statically bundled into every native build, including production** — 🟡 medium · security · _confirmed_
   - Evidence: packages/ui/src/core/Providers.tsx:18 imports localDemoOptions unconditionally, and localDemo.native.ts:2-3 imports @naya/demo-api/embedded and the fixtures. Gating is runtime-only (Providers.tsx:28 `STANDALONE_DEMO ? ...`, apiBase.ts:4-5). The seed embeds admin passwords (services/demo-api/src/seed.ts:55,303 'Naya-Admin-2026'). Fixtures are 812 KB of specimen JPGs (services/demo-api/src/fixtures.native.ts). The production EAS profile only sets EXPO_PUBLIC_DEMO=0 (apps/passenger/eas.json:33-38). apiBase.ts:4 makes demo mode the default when the env var is missing.
   - Detail: A future production build would ship the demo server, fixed-OTP logic, demo admin credentials and sample identity documents, adding bundle weight and attack surface. Demo mode is fail-open if the env flag is forgotten.
   - Fix: Swap the embedded module at build time (Metro resolver alias or a `.demo.native.ts` file chosen by an env-driven babel or metro config) so non-demo builds exclude it. Make DEMO_MODE fail closed (`=== '1'`). Add an ADR for the standalone-demo architecture.
13. **Verification claims and reference docs predate or contradict the Oct 6 state** — 🟡 medium · docs · _confirmed_
   - Evidence: README.md:166-171 (dated 30 Sep) says API 36/36, components 14/14, E2E 39/39 and no peer issues; the actual counts are 51, 15 and 40 specs, and `pnpm peers check` reports 'unmet peer react ... ^19.3.0: react-reconciler@0.34.0'. README.md:26 lists only 4 docs (omits STANDALONE-DEMO and MOBILE-FEEDBACK-*). docs/SCREENS.md (last commit 2026-09-30) has no rows for family.tsx, brand.tsx, passenger wallet.tsx or admin pages/Prototype.tsx (Services). docs/FINANCE.md and docs/INTEGRATIONS.md (Sep 30) do not mention the passenger wallet, holds and settlement, per-category commission, Cash Plus/Wafacash, SOS or family. docs/STANDALONE-DEMO.md:13 says build numbers 2/3, while apps/passenger/app.config…
   - Detail: Docs are append-only dated reports, not living references. Agents reading them get wrong numbers, missing features, and conflicting statements about what was verified.
   - Fix: Turn README verification into a short 'current baselines' section updated by CI. Update SCREENS, FINANCE and INTEGRATIONS for the Oct 6 features. Mark MOBILE-FEEDBACK-AUDIT and STANDALONE-DEMO as historical in a docs/history/ folder.
14. **E2E needs four manually started servers on specific ports; how to start them is undocumented** — 🟡 medium · dx · _confirmed_
   - Evidence: e2e/playwright.config.ts:6-7 says 'Start the servers first (see README "Tests")', but README.md:150-156 only lists `pnpm e2e`. The config expects driver at 8082 (:12), while root scripts `passenger` and `driver` (package.json:13-14) both run `expo start` on the default port 8081. There is no `webServer` entry.
   - Detail: Neither a newcomer nor an AI agent can reproduce the e2e run without tribal knowledge (e.g. `expo start --web --port 8082` and admin on 5173). This is part of why the full suite was not re-run on Oct 6.
   - Fix: Add `webServer` entries for API, passenger web (8081), driver web (8082) and admin (5173) to e2e/playwright.config.ts, or add a `pnpm e2e:servers` script, and document it in AGENTS.md.
15. **Local checkout is behind a teammate's commit on master, and the uncommitted lockfile and app.config.ts edits conflict with it** — 🟡 medium · dx · _found-by-verifier_
   - Evidence: `git status -sb` → '## master...origin/master [behind 1]'. `git log -1 origin/master` → 02ff3d3 'chore: enable unrs-resolver build in pnpm workspace configuration' (Arselene, 2026-10-06 11:51). It touches pnpm-lock.yaml (+2244), pnpm-workspace.yaml (+`unrs-resolver: true` under allowBuilds), apps/passenger/app.config.ts (buildNumber '3'→'4') and apps/driver/app.config.ts ('4'→'5'). .git/logs/refs/remotes/origin/master shows this ref arrived via a fetch on Oct 13. `git diff origin/master -- pnpm-lock.yaml \| wc -l` → 316, with different resolutions such as @napi-rs/wasm-runtime 1.2.4 vs 1.2.5 and @typescript-eslint/* 8.71.0 vs 8.71.1. The local uncommitted app.config.ts adds appleTeamId on t…
   - Detail: Two people push directly to master with no review. The user's uncommitted lockfile, workspace config and passenger config overlap a teammate commit they have not pulled. `git pull` will refuse or conflict. Committing the local lockfile, as the audit suggested, would conflict, and a careless force-push would drop the teammate's lockfile and build-number changes. Build numbers now differ in three places (docs 2/3, local 3/4, origin 4/5), which risks duplicate TestFlight uploads. Locally, pnpm-workspace.yaml also lacks the unrs-resolver allowBuilds entry that the eslint import resolver needs.
   - Fix: Stash the local changes, fast-forward to origin/master, and keep origin's pnpm-lock.yaml and pnpm-workspace.yaml. Re-apply appleTeamId and the with-ios-build-paths plugin on top of buildNumber '4', and add the plugin to the driver config. Confirm `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` passes before committing. Agree on a branch-and-PR workflow with branch protection and one source of truth for build numbers (EAS remote versioning or app.config.ts, not both), and record the current build numbers in the docs.

**Open questions raised**

- Is the full 40-test Playwright suite green on the current code? It was last fully run around Sep 30/Oct 1 (README 39/39), and the Oct 6 report contains one test. It needs a run with the API, both Expo web servers and admin started.
- Is the admin, or the demo API, deployed anywhere reachable by others (a stakeholder demo URL)? That decides whether the always-on demo-credential panel and fail-open dev mode are acceptable.
- Should apps/web be pnpm-only (delete package-lock.json), or does a hosting target (e.g. Vercel) require a standalone npm install?
- Can the docs/screens history be rewritten (force-push to ProgixDev/naya), or should LFS apply only going forward?
- Was achref-dev-skill-debt/ and its zip placed in this repo intentionally? They look like assets from an unrelated 'Skill Debt' project.
- Should the driver app also get appleTeamId and the with-ios-build-paths plugin, and will the repo stay on a path containing spaces?
- Which TestFlight builds are current? STANDALONE-DEMO.md says passenger 2 / driver 3, the configs say 3 / 4, and work/archives contains Naya-1.0.0-3.xcarchive. MOBILE-FEEDBACK-DEMO.md:48 says the native checks used copies of old binaries with refreshed JS.
- The README claim 'expo-doctor passes 21/21' was not re-verified: it needs network access and was out of scope for a read-only run.
- scenarios-feedback.spec.ts:78-79 reloads the page to close the SOS safety sheet. Is the sheet intentionally impossible to dismiss through the UI?
- Should the 'prototype' feedback features become first-class domain modules (state machines, wallet projection in packages/domain) before more work builds on them?

<a id="visual"></a>

## Visual UI/UX review from captured screens

**Health 6/10.** The core ride and money flows still look like one considered product: pearl and plum tokens, a single dominant action per state, labelled money figures with tabular numbers, semantic status banners and honest "Démo / Déplacement simulé" badges. The driver wallet, withdrawal, ride-end and debt screens are the strongest work in the repo. The October 6 feedback features (wallet, Naya Famille, SOS, categories, logo gallery, admin Services page) are visibly a different, rougher layer. They use inline `panel` styles and content-width buttons, so buttons are ragged, selection styles differ between screens, form fields replace pickers, raw coordinates show to users, and safety screens carry no danger styling. Imagery changed in the same commit: `Illustration` now ignores every raster asset and draws one generic line-art car or journey SVG. That contradicts docs/DESIGN.md, leaves about 6.8 MB of unused PNGs in the app bundle, and clashes with the website's AI-generated photos and the admin's reused generated selfies. Dark mode is mostly well tokenised, but has invisible logo preview tiles, a low-contrast switch off-state and an illegible map attribution. The four surfaces (apps, Vite admin, Next.js backoffice, landing) look only loosely like one brand: three button languages, two different backoffices, and the Rencontre symbol reading as ⌘ at small sizes. The screenshots are not a reliable source of truth. Admin captures predate the dark rail, two native/402 PNGs are blank, the "online" capture shows offline, contact sheets predate their PNGs, and no screen changed by the feedback commit has a fresh capture apart from 9 feedback shots.

**Strengths**

- Core v2 light design system is coherent on the main ride flows: pearl ground, plum reserved for actions, one full-width decision button per state (Confirmer, Accepter la course, Passer en ligne). See docs/screens/native/402/sheets/sheet-01.jpg and sheet-02.jpg.
- Money presentation on driver screens is exemplary: one hero figure, labelled sub-figures (Solde comptable / Disponible au retrait / Réservé / Dette x/150), green credits, signed debits, a before/after table on withdrawals. See docs/screens/driver/402/sheets/sheet-06.jpg and sheet-08.jpg.
- Price transparency before commitment: the Confirm button carries the total, the cancellation fee line sits under the button, and a fare-detail sheet is one tap away (docs/screens/feedback/passenger-categories-ios.png, apps/passenger/src/app/(app)/quote.tsx:192-196).
- Semantic status banners pair icon, title and sentence with one action, for example 'Plafond de dette atteint' with Recharger (docs/screens/native/402/sheets/sheet-01.jpg driver-06-debt).
- Demo honesty is visible: 'Démo', 'Trajet simulé', 'Déplacement simulé' badges and 'Environnement de démonstration · données fictives' admin footer.
- Dark palette text contrast is strong: muted #BBAFBE on surface #231D28 is 7.82:1 and accent #E5B6D4 is 9.37:1 (packages/tokens/src/index.ts:41).
- Large-text work largely holds: the offer card and wallet figures wrap rather than clip in the later captures (docs/screens/native/390-large-text/driver-07-offer.png).
- The admin uses dense, readable tables with right-aligned amounts, status pills, filters and an audit 'Chaîne intègre' indicator (docs/screens/admin/A06-rides-1440.png, A13-audit-1440.png).
- The web landing has strong editorial hierarchy and responsive behaviour from 390 to 1440 px (apps/web/review/ux-fixed-landing-desktop.jpg, ux-fixed-landing-mobile.jpg).
